## X — X part 17 part 2 + part 3 — `scripts/check-master-reads.sh`: the Master's inline reads reported from the session transcript (D-210 item 4) (M-core, 2026-09-30)

- **Why:** D-210 item 4 (GM 05:41Z «موافق — نفذ بعمق وصرامه»): "Master reads nothing inline (D-201 enforced): PR bodies, packets, backlog and CHANGELOG are read only through subagents with ≤ 20-line returns; M-core adds `scripts/check-master-reads.sh`". A PreToolUse hook cannot tell the Master's own read from its subagent's read (same session, same hooks), so the check is a transcript-based report (design default posted on #207 06:57Z, unchallenged for an hour).
- **Change:** `scripts/check-master-reads.mjs` (+ `.d.mts`, `.sh` wrapper with the name D-210 gives): `countMasterReads(entries, { maxLines })` over a Claude Code JSONL transcript — main-thread `tool_use` only (sidechain = subagent, never counted); governed = `docs/CHANGELOG.md`, `tasks/MASTER_BACKLOG.md`, `docs/notes/handover-*.md` by path suffix (absolute paths count) through `Read`, `Grep` (content mode), `Bash` (simple-command split on `;|&&||&`/newline/`()`/backticks; the read verb — cat/head/tail/less/grep/sed without an in-place option/git [-C dir] [--no-pager] show|diff — must take the governed path as an argument, so `git add`, `commit -m "$(cat <<EOF"` and `sed -i` are not reads), plus PR bodies (`gh pr view` without `--json` or with `body`; `pull_request_read` get); `m` = main-thread results over N lines (default 20) paired to a counted read or an `Agent`/`Task` call. CLI: `master-reads: <n> … <k> … <m> …` + one line per finding; exit 0 report, `--strict` exit 1 on n > 0, exit 2 usage.
- **Tests:** `tests/ops/x-part-17b.feature` + `tests/ops/tests/x-part-17b.test.ts` — fixtures per rule (absolute paths, Grep modes, `gh pr view --json state,…` not counted, Agent result over N, exit codes, wrapper) + a 300-case seeded table against an independent oracle (table-driven unit test, D-208: no fast-check outside the three scopes).
- **Defaults recorded:** report-only by default, `--strict` for a later watchdog opt-in · governed set = D-210's four sources, slice briefs excluded · `.mjs` behind the `.sh` name · not wired into the Master's watchdog yet (the watchdog prompt keeps the rule verbatim until the Master adopts the script).
- **Review (pg-reviewer, opus, D-206):** part 2 — pre-build FAIL(6: 4 blocking — Agent results in m, absolute paths, governed set, `gh pr view --json` over-reach; 2 nits) → fix round → close round 2 FAIL(6: 1 blocking — a governed path after `;|)<>&` missed; 5 nits — verb/argument pairing, trailing newline, per-occurrence `--json`, `git -C`/`--no-pager`, option/JSON/id robustness) → REVIEW CAP: no committable PASS subset without the `.mjs`, so the six findings became **part 3**, run as a fresh slice in the same worktree and landed in this one commit: pre-build FAIL(6: brief text, newline/backtick separators, `sed -i` by option token, verb boundary + leading assignments, `>`/`&` fixtures, header) → fix round → close round 2 FAIL(3 nits: redirect targets, `sed -e … -i`, heredoc/quoted text as commands) → fixed in the same round (each pinned by a case). Trailer counts part 3's two reviews.
- **Verified:** `pnpm -s test:ops` 58/58 (x-part-17b 35 scenarios) · `node --check` · eslint scripts tests/ops · ops typecheck · pre-commit ①②.
- Model: M-core session · Delegated: pg-tester (sonnet ×2), pg-builder (sonnet), pg-reviewer (opus ×2) · Review: PASS(9 findings, 2 rounds) · tokens: pg-tester ≈ 150k · pg-builder ≈ 95k · pg-reviewer ≈ 165k

## X — X part 22 — a commit subject quoting `<this-commit>` no longer turns gate ① red through PROJECT_STATE (M-core, 2026-09-30)

- **Why (Master M15 10:27Z, urgent):** commit `e600886` (#233) has a subject that quotes the angle-bracket placeholder token (the one with a space) verbatim; `scripts/scribe.mjs` copies the last five feat/fix subjects into `docs/PROJECT_STATE.md`, and `scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs --check` counts every line carrying that token as a placeholder, stale when its introducing commit is on the base ref and not HEAD → ① red on #228 (PROJECT_STATE:22), on every PR regenerating PROJECT_STATE, and on main at the next merge.
- **Change (belt and braces):** scribe renders `<this-commit>` inside a copied subject as `<this-commit>` (named constants, before truncation) · resolve-hashes skips the generated section of PROJECT_STATE from a `## Last ` heading to the next `## ` heading for `--check` and `--write` (files already on main or lane branches stop failing too); a literal in any other section is still caught. PROJECT_STATE regenerated here (the e600886 line reads `<this-commit>`).
- **Tests:** `tests/hooks/run.sh` — SC fixture commit with the space-form token in its subject → rendered as `<this-commit>`, space form absent, `--check` OK; RH fixtures in scribe's section order: space-form token inside "Last 5" (ancestor of base) → `--check` 0 and `--write` leaves it; space-form token under `## Blockers` after the Last section → `--check` 1 (pin).
- **Review (pg-reviewer, opus, D-206):** pre-build FAIL(2: fixture section order — the negative case must sit after the exempt section; a cannot-fail case folded) → fixed · close PASS(2 nits: duplicate constant, this entry's wording — fixed in the same round).
- **Verified:** `pnpm test:hooks` green · `node scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs --check` clean on the branch · eslint scripts.
- Model: M-core session · Delegated: pg-tester (sonnet ×2), pg-builder (sonnet), pg-reviewer (opus ×2) · Review: PASS(2 findings, 2 rounds) · tokens: ≈ 70k

## X — PR #147 CI: stale placeholders resolved; G16 crash root-caused to the Node 20 runner (Stryker 10 needs Node ≥ 22) (2026-09-27)

- **Why:** PR #147 (session branch → main) was red on gate ① (`resolve-hashes --check`: four unresolved this-commit placeholders in `tasks/MASTER_BACKLOG.md` left by `33a5446` on main) and on gate ⑤ (G16 "0 of 9 modules printed a 'Final mutation score' line" after ~9 s — a crash, not a score; the job output never showed the temp log).
- **Change:** placeholders resolved with `node scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs --write` (6 in `MASTER_BACKLOG.md`/`CHANGELOG.md`) · `scripts/guards-run.sh` prints the last 60 lines of the G16 log (stack frames and progress lines stripped) to stderr whenever G16 is red so CI is diagnosable · root cause: `@stryker-mutator/core` 10.0.0 declares `engines.node >=22` while every workflow pinned `node-version: 20` (the RUNBOOK's jsdom precedent, inverted) → `ci.yml` (4 jobs) and `nightly.yml` pin Node 22, root `engines.node` `>=22` (developers already run 22.x; `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` unchanged) · RUNBOOK note under the jsdom entry.
- **②③ red on the Node 22 head (green on Node 20 an hour earlier):** the job log tool returns only the last 5,000 lines and the service container printed ~5,000 lines of expected negative-test `ERROR`/`STATEMENT` pairs at job end, hiding vitest's own output; the proxy blocks the raw log download. `ci.yml` ②③/⑤ now set `log_min_messages = fatal` + `log_min_error_statement = panic` on the service container after the schema is applied (vitest already prints every failing assertion). Locally on Node 22 the whole workspace is green except `@pg-eos/documents`, which cannot launch Chrome as root in this container (not the CI failure — CI relaxes the AppArmor sandbox and runs as a normal user).
- **②③ root cause (read from the now-quiet log):** the three wave-1 contract test files imported their own package by name (`@pg-eos/contracts/billing/…`, `@pg-eos/contracts`), which Node's self-reference resolves through the `exports` map to `dist/` — present locally, absent in CI whenever turbo schedules `contracts#test` before a module's `^build` builds it (order is not fixed; the Node 20 run was green by luck). Imports rewritten to the relative `../billing/*.js` / `../index.js` form every other contracts test uses; verified green with `dist/` removed (contracts 225/225).
- **G16 in CI (per-module lines, run on `14e06a9`):** Stryker ran everywhere on Node 22, but 7 of 9 modules scored 27–67 % and identity's dry run failed, while the same code scores ≥ 75 locally: the CI dry run of wms ran 414 tests vs 1,000 locally. Cause: gate ⑤ and nightly never build `packages/*`; every test importing `@pg-eos/domain-kit`/`contracts` resolves through `exports` to a missing `dist/`, and `@stryker-mutator/vitest-runner` drops a test file whose import fails without failing the dry run (`collectTestsFromSuite` yields nothing; only `state.errorsSet` errors abort), so those mutants count as "no coverage". `scripts/mutation-all.sh` now runs `turbo run build --filter './packages/*'` first (cached no-op locally), covering guards-run G16, nightly and manual runs.
- **Default (no Node 20 locally to reproduce):** the runner bump is the default over downgrading Stryker to 9 — it aligns CI with the developer runtime and lifts the jsdom pin's reason; the next CI run of PR #147 is the evidence, the G16 tail is the fallback diagnosis.
- Model: Master session · Delegated: none · Review: n/a (bookkeeping + CI diagnostics, tool-verified: hooks 124/124, `bash -n`, `resolve-hashes --check` clean) · tokens: ~12k.

## X — OpenAPI registry populated: 72 operations from the existing contracts (enablement item 6) (2026-09-26)

- **Why:** `packages/contracts/openapi/openapi.json` was a stub (`"paths": {}`) — nothing registered into the `ContractRegistry`, so "contract tests from OpenAPI" (doc 36 §5-5) had no document to test (audit 2026-09-26, HIGH).
- **DEFAULT/RECORD/PROCEED — path convention:** no package document fixes internal API paths (doc 40 §A4 fixes only header/status rules; the client API v1 of doc 40 l.398 is a separate surface). The route is derived mechanically from the identifier every write handler already carries: `IDEMPOTENCY_ENDPOINT_* = '<module>.<use-case>.<command>'` → `POST /<module>/<use-case>/<command>`; read-only handlers → `GET /<module>/<use-case>/<handler-name>`. Nothing else invented; the GM may override the convention before the first NestJS controller binds it.
- **Change:** 24 contract files export `ROUTES`; `packages/contracts/routes.ts` aggregates, `index.ts` registers at import (zod-only, browser-safe); `_shared/route-responses.ts` holds the shared 200/400/409/422/500 (+403/404 where a handler maps them) response sets with `Problem` bodies; `openapi.json` regenerated: 68 POST + 4 GET = 72, matching the 68 identifiers. `tests/routes.test.ts`: explicit count fixture, Idempotency-Key on every POST, 4xx → `Problem` $ref, validator, byte-for-byte drift check against the committed file.
- **Not registered, on purpose:** billing (no api layer yet), sales/customer-profile + resolve-price (no handlers), platform EvaluateAlertRules (pg-boss job). Recorded defaults: GET routes carry no 409; otp-login declares no 409 (`conflict: false`) because its handler never maps IdempotencyConflictError; 403 registered exactly where a handler returns its local `HTTP_STATUS_FORBIDDEN` (hr confirm/dispute-commission, platform evaluate-alerts — corrected by the Master after the builder's literal grep found no `PROBLEM_STATUS.FORBIDDEN`).
- **Verified:** contracts lint/typecheck/test 48/48, `generate:openapi` then test again (zero drift), root lint + typecheck 28/28 (apps included).
- **Review (pg-reviewer, opus):** round 1 FAIL(4: 3 nits + 1 condition) — fidelity 68/68 handler ↔ route confirmed, nothing invented, browser-safe, tests real. Fix round (follow-up `fix(X)`): stale "no 403 registered" comments corrected; `writeErrorResponses({ conflict: false })` added and used by identity/otp-login, whose handler documents that it never maps IdempotencyConflictError (409 no longer declared there — 66 of the 68 POST routes carry 409); json regenerated; gate ① shown green in pre-commit. Trailer `Review: PASS(4 findings, 2 rounds)`.
- **DEFAULT recorded (reviewer finding 3):** the four GET routes declare `request.query` while today's framework-free handlers parse `request.body`; the future HTTP adapter (first NestJS controller) maps the query string onto the handler's input object one-to-one by field name — no handler changes until then.
- Model: Master session · Delegated: pg-builder (sonnet), pg-reviewer (opus) · Review: PASS(4 findings, 2 rounds) · tokens: builder ~145k, reviewer ~45k, Master ~15k.

## X — `PG_APP_USER` required: no superuser fallback in `packages/db` (2026-09-26)

- **Finding (audit 2026-09-26, HIGH):** `packages/db/src/client.ts:38` fell back to `PGUSER` (`postgres`, a superuser) when `PG_APP_USER` was unset — RLS and the three `current_user <> 'pgeos_app'` trigger bypasses (0034:422/608, 0036:108) were silently disabled outside CI.
- **Change:** `withContext` calls `requireAppUser()` before connecting (unset or `postgres` → throws) and `assertAppRole(client)` once per process after connecting (`select rolsuper or rolbypassrls from pg_roles where rolname = current_user` → throws for any privileged role, closing the `PGUSER=someadmin` gap the reviewer raised). Nothing runs at import, so pure domain unit tests importing the barrel still work without a database. RED test `packages/db/tests/client-app-user.test.ts` (4 cases: unset, `postgres`, a real superuser fixture role, `pgeos_app`). `docs/RUNBOOK.md`, `infra/docker/.env.example` and `scripts/check-setup.sh` (now `miss`, not NOTE) updated; remote sessions get the variable from the SessionStart hook, local shells export it.
- Verified: `packages/db` client-app-user + with-context + entity-scope.property (no DB env) green, `modules/wms/tests/unit` green without `PG_APP_USER`, eslint clean, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- Model: Master session · Delegated: pg-reviewer (opus) · Review: see ADR-0005 entry · tokens: ~15k.

## X — SessionStart hook: local PostgreSQL 16 in Claude Code on the web containers (2026-09-26)

- **Why:** the cloud session containers have PostgreSQL 16 server binaries but no Docker daemon and no running server, so every DB-backed test, `scripts/lane-db.sh`, `scripts/guards-run.sh` and gates ②③⑤ failed with `ECONNREFUSED 127.0.0.1:5432`; the three parallel lane sessions launched from the Master would have reported BLOCKED. GM directive: "نفذ فورا".
- **Delivered:** `.claude/hooks/session-start.sh` (registered as `SessionStart` in `.claude/settings.json`; exits at once unless `CLAUDE_CODE_REMOTE=true`, so local machines are untouched): initdb a cluster at `/var/lib/postgresql/16/pgeos` with the docker-compose locale (collate C, ctype C.UTF-8, SCR-TRGM-01), loopback-only trust auth like `infra/docker/docker-compose.yml`, `pg_ctl start`, create `pgeos` and apply the schema once through `database/schema/apply.sh` (never dropped, never re-applied), `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`, and export `PGHOST/PGPORT/PGUSER/PGDATABASE` plus `PG_APP_USER=pgeos_app` to the session (the same convention as `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, so tests run under RLS, not as a superuser). Idempotent; 6 s cold, <1 s warm.
- **Verified in the container:** hook run twice (second run a no-op), 183 tables in `pgeos`, `pgeos_app` role present, `bash scripts/lane-db.sh 1` created `pgeos_lane1` with guards green, `guards-run.sh` G1–G14 + G18 + G-SEED all green (G15–G17 standing NOT RUNNABLE), `modules/imile` `tests/pull-shipments` 25/25 (was `ECONNREFUSED`), eslint clean on sampled files.
- **DEFAULT/RECORD/PROCEED:** the hook runs synchronously (the session starts once Postgres is ready) — async mode would let a lane's first `psql` race the server. `.claude/*` is a frozen path during a parallel phase; this is the Master's own single-lane task, merged before the lanes are relaunched from it.
- Model: Master session · Delegated: none · Review: n/a (build tooling, no code/schema/RLS change) · tokens: ~40k.

## 2.16 part 1a-3 — OTP login endpoint (identity), DONE-in-part — 2 findings backlogged as `2.16 part 1a-3b` (2026-09-26)

- **Context — two round-1 security findings on the frozen `packages/identity` package forced a Master-owned detour before this slice could build anything.** The original brief had this slice wrap `packages/identity`'s WBS-0.17 mechanisms (`generateOtp`/`verifyOtp`/`issueSession`) directly. pg-reviewer round 1 (against that design) found: a nested-transaction pool deadlock (this endpoint's own `withIdempotentContext` transaction wrapping mechanisms that each open their OWN internal `withContext` transaction, two independent transactions nesting on one pool connection) and a hand-assembled internal RLS context (the brief's own decision 4 had workers building `WithContextCtx` context literals by hand, re-deriving trust decisions the package itself should own). Both were closed by the Master's frozen-package task **P6c**, not by this lane: part 1 (`69899c5`) added transaction-injectable siblings `generateOtpInTx`/`verifyOtpInTx`/`issueSessionInTx`; part 2 (`b18c0ca`) built the actual pre-auth flows on top of them, `requestLoginOtp(email, idem, opts?) → {expiresInMinutes, code: string|null}` and `verifyLoginOtp(email, code, idem, opts?) → {valid:false} | {valid:true, userId, sessionId, token: string|null, issuedAt, expiresAt}`, exported from `@pg-eos/identity-mechanisms`. Anti-enumeration and idempotency are now owned entirely by the package (identical response shape on every path, `token: null` marking a replay) — this lane's own workaround from decision 4 (hand-built idempotency context literals) is superseded and never shipped.
- **Delivered by this slice — the api/application composition layer only, first-ever use case in `modules/identity`:** `modules/identity/{domain,application,infrastructure,api}/otp-login/**` (domain: `InvalidOtpError`; application: `request-otp-code.ts`/`verify-otp-code.ts`/`ports.ts`/`index.ts` calling `requestLoginOtp`/`verifyLoginOtp` directly, no `UserDirectoryPort`/`OtpExpiryPort`/repository left to adapt — deleted post-P6c rewrite; infrastructure: pino logger adapter; api: `composition.ts`/`handlers.ts`, same framework-free handler shape as `modules/wms/api/receive-inbound`), contract `packages/contracts/identity/otp-login.ts`. ## 3.13 part 5a — dispute-side DB backstop on `hr.guard_commission_daily_status()`, migration 0035 (hr), DONE-in-part — 6 slice-close findings accepted as-is (2026-09-26)

- **Delivered:** closes item (1) of the 3 findings 3.13 part 4's slice-close review deferred (REVIEW CAP, no round 3) — `hr.guard_commission_daily_status()` had a DB-level actor/SoD check only on the edge reaching `confirmed`; the `calculated → disputed` edge had none, so a raw SQL UPDATE bypassing `DisputeCommission`'s own application-layer `isOwnRow()` check could dispute another employee's row. Migration `database/migrations/0035_3_commission-daily-dispute-sod.sql` extends the function (verbatim restatement of 0033's body, one new block added) with `not exists (select 1 from identity.users u where u.id = platform.current_user_id() and u.employee_id = old.employee_id) → raise insufficient_privilege (42501)` on that edge — bound to **`old.employee_id`**, not `new.employee_id` (pre-migration review round 1 finding 2: binding to `new.employee_id` would let an actor linked to employee X update employee Y's row, setting both `employee_id = X` and `status = 'disputed'` in the same statement, and pass the check). No new column, no new permission, `security definer`/`search_path` unchanged. ## 2.12 part 3 — order_lines.picked_by/picked_at + full self-check signal (wms), DONE — closes SCR-WMS-OUT-03; part 4 (Pack/Load) next (2026-09-26)

- **Delivered:** closes the residual self-check gap 2.12 part 2's round 2 surfaced — a picker whose ONLY action was a zero-quantity pick on a line OTHER than the one that completed the order could still pass `CheckOrder`'s self-check (no ledger row posted, `outbound_orders.picked_by` not set). Ruled by **D-190 option a** (GM-delegated evaluation session, relayed by the Master; recorded verbatim on the D-190 row in `docs/DECISION_LOG.md`): `wms.order_lines` gains `picked_by uuid` + `picked_at timestamptz`, set by EVERY `PickLine` call including zero-qty (`pick-line.ts` reuses the single `occurredAt = deps.clock.now()` already computed for the audit row — never a second clock call in one command). `CheckOrder`'s self-check drops the old `actorId === order.pickedBy` comparison entirely and becomes `hasAnyLinePickedBy(orderId, actorId) || hasPickMovementByActor(orderId, actorId)` — a new repo method OR'd with part 2's own check. `wms.outbound_orders.picked_by` itself is UNCHANGED (still the last-completing-picker column, just no longer read by the self-check).
- **Migration `0032_1_order-lines-picked-by.sql`** (applied): two additive nullable columns, a DB-level pairing CHECK (`(picked_by is null) = (picked_at is null)`, mirrors `0010`'s `variance_photo_pair` precedent), two `identity.column_classification` rows (`sensitivity = 'public'`, matching `outbound_orders.picked_by`/`checked_by`/`packed_by`). No new RLS statement — `wms.order_lines` already carries RLS via 13B's `internal_only` auto-policy (no `entity_id` on the table); the new columns inherit it. Known consequence, no backfill: an order already `picking`/`picked` when this migration applies keeps `picked_by`/`picked_at = null` on lines touched before the migration — acceptable, pilot runs on seed 019 + synthetic data only (D-127).
- **Review trail — pre-migration gate:** round 1 FAIL(4: false RLS claim in the header/brief/SCR — `order_lines` actually HAS RLS via 13B's auto-policy `internal_only`, not RLS-less as first claimed; G-01 authority not yet on record; no DB-level pairing CHECK; no note on already-mid-pick orders) → one fix round → round 2 PASS(0).
- **Review trail — slice-close gate:** round 1 FAIL(5: 2 stale doc comments in `errors.ts`/`invariants.ts` describing the old `picked_by`-comparison rule; 1 test-coverage gap — no test proved the line stamp on a NORMAL non-zero pick, or that `picked_at` matches the audit row's `occurred_at`; findings 4-5 were pg-scribe bookkeeping items, not code defects) → one fix round → round 2 PASS(0).

## X — SCR-HR-COMM-01 approved: commission read/confirm permission split (2026-09-26)

- `hr.commission.read_all` gated two unrelated commission tables — found by the 0033 pre-migration review; split per D-190. `hr.driver_commission.read_all` (new) now gates `hr.commission_daily`'s own `own_commission` SELECT policy; `hr.commission.read_all` keeps gating `hr.sales_commission_events` only, untouched. A new `hr.commission.confirm` write permission (DEL_SUP) authorizes resolving a commission dispute, checked via `platform.has_perm()` and scoped to the `disputed → confirmed` transition only.
- **Mutation-proof substitute, for the record (revised after slice-close review round 1):** the Master asked for a literal mutation proof on the disputed-path `hr.commission.confirm` check — remove it from the live trigger, confirm the relevant test goes RED, restore it, confirm GREEN. Running that via raw SQL against `pgeos_lane3` (temporarily replacing `hr.guard_commission_daily_status()` without the check) was refused by this session's own tool-permission classifier as "weakening a security control," even on an isolated, non-shared per-lane database — the refusal was respected, not routed around through another tool or session. First substitute attempt (superseded): granting `hr.commission.confirm` to a real shared role (`SALES_MGR`) inside a test, with the grant deleted in the test body's own `finally` — slice-close review round 1 correctly flagged this as a D-183 violation (DELETE belongs only in the suite's own `afterAll`) and as unnecessary risk to concurrent lane sessions if `PGDATABASE` ever fell back to the shared `pgeos`; removed entirely. Final proof, in place: a raw `withAppRole` UPDATE that bypasses the application command layer completely, run by an actor who holds `hr.driver_commission.read_all` but NOT `hr.commission.confirm`, attempting `disputed → confirmed` directly against `hr.commission_daily` — asserted refused by the TRIGGER's own specific error/SQLSTATE, proving the DB backstop is independently load-bearing regardless of what the application layer does; paired with an integration test asserting `confirmCommission()` itself throws the specific typed `ConfirmPermissionRequiredError` for the same actor, and a handler test asserting its 403 mapping. No live grant to a real role was ever needed or made.

Doc-38 row 2.12's own acceptance line — "Self-check rejected" — is met and confirmed safe at both integration and handler test level. **2.12 part 1 DONE-in-part; part 2 open (4 items) — 2.12 DONE when part 2 lands.**
- **Architecture fix (round 1 finding 1):** `PickLine`'s stock handling originally used hand-written SQL bypassing the shared ledger (`postMovementInTx`). Fixed by adding `modules/wms/infrastructure/process-outbound/ledger.ts`, a `LedgerPort` adapter mirroring `infrastructure/receive-inbound/ledger.ts`'s own pattern exactly — restores the shared rebuild-key lock, balance advisory lock, `validateEntry`, `NegativeStockError` mapping and the per-movement outbox+audit pairing (G9).
- **Review trail (P7/D-186 review cap):** round 1 FAIL (9 findings, all in PickLine's stock-ledger handling: no shared ledger, a double-pick gap, unbounded pick quantity, a stuck partial reservation, a zero-qty ledger violation, an unreserved-line pick accepted, an untyped 500 on unknown lineId, 2 test-coverage gaps) → one fix round (pg-backend fixed findings 1-7 incl. the new ledger adapter; pg-tester fixed findings 8-9 with RED→GREEN proof tests for all 5 bug scenarios, plus one legitimate test-assertion change — idempotent-replay outbox count 1→2, a direct consequence of the new ledger's own G9 outbox pairing) → round 2 (final) FAIL (3 findings, all confirmed separable and non-blocking). Per REVIEW CAP (D-186/P7): round 2 FAIL commits and merges only the GREEN, PASS-reviewed subset; every open finding becomes part-2 scope, no round 3.
- **2.12 part 2 scope (4 deferred items, same `wms/process-outbound` lock, next task):**
  1. No property/unit tests for 3 new pure invariants (`assertPickedWithinReserved`, `assertLineReservedForPick`, `assertLineNotAlreadyPicked`) — code confirmed correct, test-only gap (pg-tester).
  2. `handleCheckOrder` missing its own 400 (no Idempotency-Key) / 409 (StaleVersionError) tests — code confirmed correct, test-only gap (pg-tester).
  3. A real logic gap: a zero-qty repeat pick with a reason on an UNRESERVED line (`location_id` null) is never rejected by the double-pick guard (only the reserved-line zero-pick case is covered) — can be called repeatedly, bumping version and writing audit rows, though it never touches stock/ledger/outbox; also a wrong error message on the reserved-zero-pick path. **Needs both pg-backend (logic fix) and pg-tester (coverage)**, not pg-tester alone.
  4. Master-ruled (separate from round 2): `wms.outbound_orders.picked_by` records only the LAST picker on a multi-person pick, so an earlier picker could still pass `CheckOrder`'s self-check on the final call — a real gap in "Self-check rejected" for multi-picker orders. No schema change needed (every pick already posts a `wms.stock_movements` row with `performed_by` via the ledger). Fix: the self-check rule becomes "the checker must not be the `performed_by` of ANY pick movement on this order", not just the single `picked_by` column.
- **`tests/receive-inbound` on the new isolated `pgeos_lane1` database (Master-directed, separate item this session):** 183/183 clean; the 8 failures previously seen on the shared database are confirmed orphaned `wms.locations` fixture rows left over from before the one-DB-per-lane migration (P4a), not real defects. No code/test change made.

## X — P5: RLS isolation matrix (2026-09-26)

- New `tests/isolation/tests/rls-matrix.test.ts` (pg-tester, Master task P5): a CATALOG-DRIVEN RLS
  matrix, replacing the drift risk of a hand-maintained table list. It enumerates, live from
  `pg_catalog` (never a hard-coded list), every base/partitioned-parent table carrying `entity_id`
  in the fourteen business schemas — **79 tables** on this run — and for each asserts (1)
  `relrowsecurity` is true, and (2) its entity/ownership-scoping policy is real and correctly
  shaped, via `it.each` (one named case per table).
- Behavioral check (3) — an admin-seeded entity-B row is invisible to a `pgeos_app` caller scoped
  to entity A, plus a positive control (an admin-seeded entity-A row IS visible) — runs wherever a
  fully generic fixture row can be constructed from `pg_catalog` alone (NOT NULL/no-default columns
  resolved via single-column FK lookups against EXISTING rows, or type-keyed generic literals;
  never fabricated business/reference data). Of the 69 tables eligible for this path: **33 got the
  full behavioral proof, all green — no RLS entity-isolation leak found**; **36 landed in the
  visible `SEEDING_EXCEPTIONS` list** (own `it.each`, one case per table quoting the database's own
  rejection reason) — mostly an empty reference table this file will not fabricate rows into
  (`sales.accounts`/`hr.employees` hold zero seed rows on a fresh `apply.sh` run), a composite or
  missing single-column primary key (`identity.user_entities`, `platform.audit_log`), a composite
  foreign key, an unsupported column type (e.g. `time without time zone`), or a genuine CHECK
  constraint (SQLSTATE 23514) this file correctly does not know the business meaning of.
- **Finding, not a new defect:** 10 of the 79 tables carry NO policy literally named `entity_scope`
  — 8 reference/lookup tables (`billing.gl_accounts`, `catalog.services`, `hr.org_units`,
  `hr.teams`, `platform.counters`, `platform.document_templates`, `platform.settings`,
  `wms.warehouses`, governed by `reference_read`/`reference_write`, `platform.is_internal()`-gated),
  plus `hr.commission_daily` (`own_commission`) and `platform.idempotency_keys`
  (`idem_entity_scope` + `idem_own`). Verified against `database/schema/guards.sql`'s own G7
  comment: an earlier, broader guard DID flag exactly this shape ("a table with `entity_id` but no
  `entity_scope` policy") and was deliberately narrowed under D-133 because it reported "9
  reference/specially-designed tables by 13B's own design". This matrix respects that prior
  decision — `NAMED_ENTITY_SCOPE_EXCEPTIONS` asserts each of the 10 tables' real, documented
  alternative policy exists instead of a nonexistent `entity_scope`, and excludes them from the
  generic entity-boundary behavioral proof (their access control is not a plain entity boundary).
  No schema change requested; no new G-01 filed.
- `tests/isolation/tests/app-role-rls.test.ts`: `ENTITY_SCOPE_POLICY_COUNT` left at 74, unedited —
  only a comment added pointing to the new matrix as the drift-proof, catalog-driven counterpart.
- Independent safety net kept alongside the `it.each`: a direct catalog query mirroring G7's own
  `relkind in ('r','p')` scan (including partitions this time, unlike the file's own per-table
  enumeration) asserts zero `entity_id`-bearing tables lack RLS — 0 rows.
- **Master addition, same commit — view `security_invoker` matrix, D-133 ruling:** a second
  catalog-driven `it.each` in the same file, one named case per view (`pg_class.relkind = 'v'`) in
  the fourteen operational schemas — **16 views**. Rule = **D-133** (migration
  `0007_M_pgeos-app-role-entity-scope.sql:12-17`, LEAN DESIGN — views are not rewritten; pgeos_app
  is granted no privilege at all on any view, and `guards.sql`'s own extended G7 already flags a
  view only when pgeos_app still holds a privilege on it without `security_invoker=true`). An
  earlier draft of this check asked for `security_invoker=true` unconditionally (or a cited
  exception) on every view — that contradicted the already-approved D-133 design and is corrected
  here before landing; the **G19 guard proposal this earlier ruling implied is WITHDRAWN** — G7
  (extended, D-133) already covers the real risk, no new guard needed. Corrected rule: for each
  view, IF `pgeos_app` holds ANY privilege on it, THEN `reloptions` must carry
  `security_invoker=true`; a view with no `pgeos_app` privilege passes outright, its case name
  stating "… — not granted — D-133". `VIEW_INVOKER_EXCEPTIONS` (the earlier design) is removed —
  no longer needed. Result on this run: **16 views, 1 granted** (`wms.space_dashboard`, which
  already carries `security_invoker=true` — migration `0012_M_space-dashboard-invoker-grant.sql`)
  — **all 16 green**, including `wms.client_space_overview` (not granted to `pgeos_app` either,
  despite carrying the option itself for its own separate reason —
  13B-Schema-Reference-Consolidation.sql:4669-4670) and the 14 other, ungranted views.
- Verified: `pnpm guards:run` (against a throwaway `pgeos_p5` database built via a fresh
  `apply.sh`, never `pgeos`/`pgeos_lane*`) — **G1–G14, G18, G-SEED all green** (G14 = the full
  isolation suite, all three spec files green); `bash tests/hooks/run.sh` — **111/111**. G15–G17
  remain NOT RUNNABLE (pre-existing, unrelated to this task).

## X — P4a: one database per lane worktree (2026-09-26)

- **Why:** the three lane worktrees (`../pg-eos-lane-1/2/3`) shared one database, `pgeos`, in the local Docker container, so their test fixtures polluted each other. P4a gives each lane its own database in the SAME container, never a separate container.
- `scripts/lane-db.sh <1|2|3>` (new): creates `pgeos_lane<id>` in the running container if absent (CREATE DATABASE only, never drops), applies the schema via the existing `database/schema/apply.sh` (reused, not reimplemented) only on that first creation — `apply.sh` itself cannot be safely re-run on an already-populated database without `--recreate`, which this script will not do — and writes `infra/docker/.env` (git-ignored, `.gitignore` already covers `.env`/`.env.*`) with `PGDATABASE=pgeos_lane<id>`, preserving every other key already there. Idempotent: verified by creating `pgeos_lane1` for real against the running container, then re-running — second run leaves the database and schema untouched and only rewrites the `.env` key.
- `infra/docker/.env.example`: documents the new PGDATABASE convention (Master keeps `pgeos`; a lane runs `lane-db.sh` to get its own).
- Audit of scripts/, `database/*.sh`, `packages/db` config and the `tests/isolation` / `tests/ops` vitest configs found every one of them already reads `${PGDATABASE:-pgeos}` (shell) or `process.env['PGDATABASE'] ?? 'pgeos'` (TS) — no hard-coded literal needed changing; the only remaining hard-coded `pgeos` in scripts is `.claude/hooks/db-guard.sh`'s D-183 shared-database check, explicitly out of scope for this task (see the D-188 note below).
- `scripts/check-setup.sh`: new "Lane database isolation" check (§0) — decides on the **process env only** (not `infra/docker/.env`): packages/db, the vitest configs, `apply.sh` and `guards-run.sh` all read `PGDATABASE` from the environment, and nothing sources `infra/docker/.env` automatically, so a lane whose `.env` says `pgeos_lane<id>` but whose shell has no `PGDATABASE` would still run every test against the shared `pgeos` while looking isolated — caught in Master review of the first commit and fixed here. Refuses (non-zero) from a lane worktree (basename `pg-eos-lane-1/2/3`) whose process `PGDATABASE` is unset or `pgeos`; the `.env` file is read only to sharpen the MISS message (names the gap when `.env` already holds the lane's database), and the message gives the durable fix: add `"env": {"PGDATABASE": "pgeos_lane<id>"}` to that worktree's `.claude/settings.local.json` (git-ignored, confirmed by `.gitignore`; Claude Code applies it to every Bash command), or export it in the shell. The Master checkout (`pg-eos-gov` / `claude-kit`) is unaffected and keeps `pgeos`.
- `scripts/lane-db.sh` and `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1: same durable-fix instruction (`.claude/settings.local.json` env entry or shell export) added to the script's final message and to the RUNBOOK paragraph, so writing `infra/docker/.env` is never mistaken for isolation by itself.
- `docs/RUNBOOK.md` §1: worktree → database table (`pg-eos-gov`/`claude-kit` → `pgeos`; `pg-eos-lane-N` → `pgeos_laneN`) and the one setup command; notes the MEMORY rule (§2) stays until measured otherwise — this change removes fixture collisions, not host memory pressure.
- `tests/hooks/run.sh`: +6 cases for the check-setup.sh refusal (lane worktree + default/explicit `pgeos` → refused; lane with process env set → not refused; lane with ONLY `infra/docker/.env` set, no process env → refused; lane with both process env and `.env` set → not refused; Master worktree → not refused) — **97/97** (was 91/91).
- D-188 note: the Master's `db-guard.sh` cleanup-exception marker (shared `pgeos`) retires once all three lanes run on their own database — a Master task after this merges, not done here (`.claude/hooks/db-guard.sh` untouched, as directed).
- Master, on rebase: `resolve-hashes --write` resolved the 2.11 part 5 placeholder in PROJECT_STATE to `2cfa3eb` (P3) instead of `f9aeecc` — blame returns the last commit that edited the line, not the one that introduced the placeholder. Corrected by hand here; resolver fix (walk `git log -L` to the commit that first carried the placeholder) is open as `X part 2` in PROJECT_STATE Blockers.

## X — P3: hash resolver, CI check, governance ratio (2026-09-26)

- `scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs`: resolves the literal placeholder text (angle-bracket `this commit`,
  CLAUDE.md · GIT) in `docs/PROJECT_STATE.md`, `tasks/MASTER_BACKLOG.md`, `tasks/LANE_LOCKS.md`,
  `docs/CHANGELOG.md`. For every line still carrying it, `git blame --porcelain -L n,n` finds the
  introducing commit on whatever is currently checked out (never a fixed ref — a rebase-merge
  rewrites lane hashes). `--write` replaces the placeholder with that commit's 7-char short hash (a
  line blame attributes to uncommitted work — the all-zero hash — is left alone). `--check` makes no
  edits and exits 1 listing `file:line` for every STALE placeholder: one whose introducing commit
  (a) is an ancestor of the base ref (`$RESOLVE_BASE`, default `origin/main`, falls back to `main`,
  0-with-a-note if neither exists) AND (b) is not HEAD — not simply "!= HEAD" (Master review round 1
  BLOCKER: on a `pull_request` build the checkout is a synthetic merge commit, so a naive "!= HEAD"
  check would attribute every lane PR's own new placeholders to their lane commits and turn every
  lane PR red; restricting staleness to placeholders already reachable from base means a PR's own
  new placeholders pass and old ones already on base still get caught).
- CI gate ① (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `static` job): new step `node scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs --check`;
  its checkout gets `fetch-depth: 0` (blame/merge-base need full history, not the default shallow
  clone). Ran `--write` once in this commit so main starts clean: **24 stale placeholders resolved**
  across `docs/PROJECT_STATE.md` (2), `tasks/MASTER_BACKLOG.md` (16, one line carried two),
  `tasks/LANE_LOCKS.md` (7) — mostly `8d2ead9` (WBS 3.1 part 2) and earlier lane-part commits never
  followed up by a next-task commit; `docs/CHANGELOG.md` had none outstanding. `--check` verified
  clean afterward.
- **Missing wire (Master review round 1):** the GIT rule ("the previous task's commit hash is
  recorded in PROJECT_STATE inside the NEXT task's commit") needs someone to actually run the
  resolver. `.claude/commands/slice.md` step 11 and `.claude/agents/pg-scribe.md`'s ROLE list both
  now say to run `node scripts/resolve-hashes.mjs --write` after `git rebase origin/main` and before
  staging (AGENT CONSTRAINTS block untouched, byte-identical, md5 `feb08014` in all five agent files).
- `scripts/gov-ratio.sh`: prints, for `${GOV_BUDGET_REF:-origin/main}` over the last 7 days — total commits, `feat/fix(<WBS>)` count (WBS ≠ X, the commits commit-msg requires a Review trailer on), that ratio as a percent against the ≥ 60% GOVERNANCE BUDGET target, `chore(X)`/`docs(X)` count per calendar day, and the average review rounds across those feat/fix commits, parsed via `scripts/lib/review-trailer.sh`'s `review_trailer_line`/`review_trailer_form`/`review_rounds` (old-form trailers carry no round count and are excluded from the average — reported separately, along with feat/fix commits with no Review trailer at all). Pure bash + git + awk, `set -euo pipefail`, no python/node dependency — runs the same under Git Bash on Windows and in CI.
- `.claude/commands/pg-state.md`: step 4 now runs `bash scripts/gov-ratio.sh` and prints its output verbatim, replacing the old inline `git log`/count one-liner.
- `.claude/commands/pg-resume.md` and `pg-state.md`: both now read ONLY `docs/PROJECT_STATE.md`, `tasks/LANE_LOCKS.md` and the module brief `.claude/briefs/<module>.brief.md` (CLAUDE.md · OPERATING RULES · START/BRIEFS: "PROJECT_STATE.md ... already carries what the slice needs"). pg-resume no longer reads `CLAUDE.md` (already loaded as project instructions) or `tasks/MASTER_BACKLOG.md` (the "Next 3 tasks" section of PROJECT_STATE already carries what's runnable); `allowed-tools` trimmed to match (Grep/Glob dropped from pg-resume).

Rather than an `eslint-disable` (banned, AGENT CONSTRAINTS) or a new `globals` dependency, added one scoped config block declaring just `process: 'readonly'` for `scripts/**/*.mjs`.

## 3.1 (part 2) — AssertVehicleAssignable: the real hard gate — DONE (2026-09-26)

- **Part 2 of WBS 3.1, completing the row. Master ruling (2026-09-26, recorded below) closes the row DONE.** `AssertVehicleAssignable` — the real INV-C4-1 hard gate ("Expired-doc vehicle cannot be assigned"), deferred out of part 1. Fails closed on `ctx.isInternal` before reading `tms.vehicle_documents`, since RLS silently empties that read for a non-internal session rather than erroring it — checking `ctx.isInternal` explicitly first is what actually protects the gate. Reuses part 1's `expiredDocumentsOf` predicate (the single comparison implementation, never duplicated) and its `VehicleDocumentAccessDeniedError` class (now legitimately used on both the original write-path RLS-rejection translation and this new read-path proactive refusal — docstring corrected to describe both). Throws `VehicleNotAssignableError` with `i18nKey` `'fleet.vehicle.notAssignable'` and structured params (`plateNo`, `expiredDocuments`), surfaced in the 422 response. No Idempotency-Key — pure read-and-assert, no write, no side effect. `modules/fleet/application/register-vehicle/register-vehicle.ts`'s `VEHICLE_REGISTERED_EVENT_TYPE` now typed `CatalogedEventType` (imported from `@pg-eos/events`) now that `fleet.vehicle.registered` landed in `packages/events/catalog.ts` after part 1 merged.
- Files: `modules/fleet/{domain,application,infrastructure,api,tests}/assert-vehicle-assignable/**`; `modules/fleet/domain/register-vehicle/errors.ts` (docstring only); `modules/fleet/application/register-vehicle/register-vehicle.ts` (event-type typing only); `modules/fleet/index.ts` (barrel exports); `packages/contracts/fleet/assert-vehicle-assignable.ts` + `packages/contracts/package.json` (export entry); `docs/notes/slice-briefs/_slice-3.1-part2.brief.md` (7 files / 530 lines, under the 8-file/1,000-line P7 cap).
- Tests: unit + integration + a genuine security regression test (the non-internal-actor fixture gets a real `identity.user_entities` row for the vehicle's own entity, so the vehicle itself stays visible and only its documents are hidden by RLS — proving the fail-closed `ctx.isInternal` check is what's actually protecting the gate, not an unrelated 404).
- **Review trail — P7 rules, two-round cap:** round 1 FAIL(6, all brief-compliance/citation-hygiene class, no architectural rework — carried from the original round-2 FAIL that split part 2 off part 1). Fixed: a fabricated brief citation across three files (corrected to quote the brief accurately or state the design decision plainly, no false attribution); a stale docstring in `domain/assert-vehicle-assignable/errors.ts` naming the wrong reused predicate; the same stale docstring in the test file header; `register-vehicle/errors.ts`'s `VehicleDocumentAccessDeniedError` docstring updated to describe both its write-path and new read-path uses without contradiction; the security regression test fixed to give the non-internal actor a real `user_entities` row (finding 5 above); the two out-of-Read-ONLY precedent citations resolved by adding them to the brief's own Read ONLY list. Round 2: PASS (0 open).
- **Master ruling on doc 38 row 3.1's acceptance criterion (2026-09-26), recorded verbatim, not softened:** "Row 3.1 is DONE, not 'DONE-in-part': its acceptance 'Expired-doc vehicle cannot be assigned' is proven where it can be proven today — the gate AssertVehicleAssignable refuses with a test; enforcing it at the assignment write belongs to 3.4 (delivery tasks/routes write vehicle_id, 01:890/907) and to the G-01 DB-level guard filed with part 1." Nothing in this codebase yet CALLS `AssertVehicleAssignable` from a real assignment write path (`tms.delivery_tasks.vehicle_id` / `tms.routes.vehicle_id`), because the delivery module that will do that doesn't exist yet — same G-01 note already filed in `tasks/backlog/MIGRATION-REQUEST-3.md` at part 1, now also carried as an explicit "TODO — must call fleet AssertVehicleAssignable (3.1)" line on WBS 3.4's own MASTER_BACKLOG row. ## 3.1 (part 1) — Register vehicle + documents — NOT DONE (2026-09-25)

- Scope (recorded): part 1 of 3.1 only — `RegisterVehicle`, insert-only registration of a `tms.vehicles` row plus its `tms.vehicle_documents` rows in the same transaction. No migration — `tms.vehicles`/`tms.vehicle_documents` already exist with `entity_scope`/`internal_only` RLS (verified live against `pg_policies`, not just static schema files). - **CI caught a duplicate `LocationBlockedError` class after review PASS** (PR #67 gate ②③ red on the ephemeral DB): `domain/process-outbound/errors.ts` defined its own `LocationBlockedError` (condition 7, `OutboundCheckError`-based, i18n-keyed) with the same name as the pre-existing, unrelated `src/stock-ledger/errors.ts`'s `LocationBlockedError` (put-away structural block, plain `Error`) — both re-exported from the same module barrel, breaking `instanceof` for the older class (`tests/integration/location-limits.test.ts`'s put-away-into-a-blocked-location scenario regressed). Neither pg-reviewer's four rounds nor the isolated `process-outbound` suite caught it, since only a full, unfiltered `wms` module test run exercises both call sites together. Fixed one-shot (Master-authorized, not counted against the review-round cap): renamed the process-outbound class to `OutboundLocationBlockedError` (i18nKey unchanged, `wms.outbound.check.locationBlocked`); `src/stock-ledger/errors.ts` untouched. Amended into this commit rather than a separate one, per the Master's non-destructive-squash convention for this slice; pushed as `lane/1-r3`. **Process note for future slices:** a fix-round or closing verification should run the module's FULL test suite at least once, not only the touched use case's own filtered suite — a name collision across use cases inside one module is invisible to a filtered run.

## 3.17 (part 1) — DTL live audit engine: gate evaluation mechanism — NOT DONE (2026-09-25)

- Scope (recorded): part 1 of 3.17 only — `EvaluateDtlProblem`, the gate-evaluation mechanism for a raised DTL problem. **G0 (completeness) is the only gate built as mechanical domain logic** — doc 07 §5-2's own words: "ناقص → بشري فوراً" (incomplete → human immediately); completeness = evidence_urls non-empty AND (customer_text OR driver_text present) AND raised_at present; a G0 failure short-circuits to `engine_decision = 'human'`, no content-analysis port call, `engine_confidence` stays `null` (nothing in doc 07 §5-2 or doc 40 supplies a number to fabricate one from). **G1-G4 are entirely delegated to a `DtlContentAnalysisPort`, never reimplemented as invented business rules** — G1 (type/evidence conflict), G2 (OCR with sender discrimination), G3 (customer decision written in the driver's handwriting — doc 07 calls this "أخطر حالة", the most critical case) and G4 (driver-responsiveness from route/trajectory, no numeric threshold anywhere in the package) all genuinely require content understanding this slice cannot honestly build as domain rules; the engine trusts the port's decision verbatim, same pattern as `PullShipments` trusting its portal port. The command never sets `closed_by` (ADR-27 auto-close needs a `level = 1` row in `imile.dtl_rule_autonomy`, unreachable today — no promotion mechanism exists); insert-only into `imile.dtl_problems`, no version column, no update path (§5-3's own future slice); one `platform.audit_log` row per insert, `changed_fields` narrowed to what was actually written; Idempotency-Key required at the API layer, same 400/409/200 shape as `pull-shipments`. Doc 38's full 3.17 acceptance is NOT met — still needs the §5-3 auditor screen, the nightly reconciliation job at 01:40, ADR-27 `dtl_rule_autonomy` promotion/auto-close, and a real OCR/handwriting-analysis vendor for G1-G4 (none chosen).
- Files (all new unless noted): `modules/imile/{domain,application,infrastructure,api,tests}/evaluate-dtl-problem/**` (from `scripts/new-slice.sh`); `packages/contracts/imile/evaluate-dtl-problem.ts`; `packages/contracts/package.json` (script-mechanism export registration only, same sanctioned Master-tier precedent as `pull-shipments`/3.14 part 1); `modules/imile/index.ts` (additive named exports: `evaluateDtlProblem`, its types, `PortNotConfiguredError`, `InvalidPortResultError`, `MissingActorError` aliased); `docs/notes/slice-briefs/_slice-3.17.brief.md` (D-186 self-enforced budget: ≤ 8 files/≤ 1,000 lines, corrected across review rounds); `docs/notes/2026-09-24-imile-agent-scenario.md` (two new G-01 rows — j: no OCR/handwriting-analysis vendor chosen, gates G1-G4 out of scope; k: `imile.dtl_problems` has no `entity_id`, no outbox write). No migration this slice — no schema change; `imile.dtl_problems` already exists, insert-only so no version column needed.
- Tests: pg-tester wrote RED first — `modules/imile/tests/evaluate-dtl-problem/{evaluate-dtl-problem.feature,evaluate-dtl-problem.test.ts,invariants.property.test.ts,handlers.test.ts}` — 4 Gherkin scenarios (G0-fail → human, no port call; complete problem evaluated end to end through the port with its result stored unmodified; port itself decides `reject`, `closed_by` stays null; port not configured → `PortNotConfiguredError` mapped to 500, no row written); pg-backend built to GREEN, confirmed by pg-tester and pg-reviewer.
- G-01 filed, not resolved (`docs/notes/2026-09-24-imile-agent-scenario.md` §4): **row j** — `EvaluateDtlProblem`'s G1-G4 gates need a `DtlContentAnalysisPort`; no OCR/handwriting-analysis vendor has been chosen yet, same class as row (d)/D-149's own portal-adapter gap; `NotConfiguredDtlContentAnalysisPort` is the production default and throws `PortNotConfiguredError`. **row k** — `imile.dtl_problems` has no `entity_id` column (same class as rows e/g on `imile.agent_health`/`imile.shipments`), so this slice's own insert is not published to `platform.outbox` — same precedent as WBS 2.6 `wms.skus`, closed D-116: no invented `platform.*` workaround, no `entity_id` added without a ruling.
- Review trail: **4 review rounds, 27 findings fixed.** Round 1 FAIL(13) — largest; included a real gap in `Write ONLY` scope (module barrel `index.ts` and `packages/contracts/package.json` and the scenario-note file missing from the brief's own write list, corrected in the brief), plus domain/contract/repository defects and missing negative-path coverage. Round 2 FAIL(12) — narrower defects and further brief-write-scope corrections (finding 11: the scenario-note G-01 rows were also missing from `Write ONLY`). Round 3: **D-186's two-worker-fix-round cap was hit** — the 2 remaining findings were fixed by the Master directly at SESSION tier (sonnet), not re-delegated to a worker for a third round and not escalated to opus as D-117 literally names for this tier of escalation — pg-reviewer flagged the tier deviation in its round-4 report but did not block on it, since both fixes were straightforward and fully re-verifiable. Round 4 (opus): PASS(0 findings) — both round-3 fixes independently re-verified clean.
- **D-186 honesty note, recorded verbatim, not softened:** this slice ran under GM directive D-186 (self-enforced ≤ 8-file/≤ 1,000-line brief and a two-worker-review-round cap). The cap was hit at round 3; per D-186 the next fix round goes to the Master directly rather than back to a worker, which is what happened — but the tier the Master used (session sonnet) deviates from D-117's literal wording, which names opus for this class of escalation. This is the same class of deviation 3.14 part 2 recorded for its own round-count overrun, and it is recorded here the same way: plainly, not smoothed over. Round 4's opus pg-reviewer re-verification of both fixes is the mitigation, not a retroactive excuse.
- Model: sonnet (pg-tester, pg-backend, pg-scribe) · opus (pg-reviewer, every review pass) · session sonnet (Master direct fixes, round 3 — D-186's two-round cap was hit; D-117 names opus for this tier of escalation, this session ran it at sonnet instead, a recorded deviation, both fixes re-verified by pg-reviewer/opus in round 4) · Delegated: pg-tester, pg-backend, pg-reviewer, pg-scribe · Review: PASS(27 findings fixed across 4 rounds)

## X — D-183: brief breach by pg-tester (lane 1, 2.11 part 1) recorded; DB-delete rule in every agent file; `db-guard.sh` PreToolUse hook — DONE (2026-09-25)

- Breach (pg-reviewer round 2 finding 12, WBS 2.11 part 1): the pg-tester worker deleted its leaked `_procout_handlers_*` fixture rows directly on the shared `pgeos` through psql after both the lane-1 session and the Master had declined; the harness flagged it as an Auto-Mode Bypass. Procedural breach, zero harm — the GM's deletion mandate for exactly those rows was already on its way (D-183 item 1).
- Rule added verbatim to `.claude/agents/pg-backend|pg-frontend|pg-reviewer|pg-scribe|pg-tester.md`: «لا DELETE / DROP / TRUNCATE على القاعدة المشتركة خارج afterAll لحزمة الاختبار نفسها؛ صف غريب يُبلَّغ للـ Master ولا يُمسّ».
- Mechanism (D-179 style): `.claude/hooks/db-guard.sh`, PreToolUse on Bash (`.claude/settings.json`), refuses any `psql` command carrying `delete from` / `drop ` / `truncate ` whose target is `pgeos` (`-d`/`--dbname`, inline `PGDATABASE=`, or the session's `PGDATABASE`); heredoc bodies are inspected; throwaway databases, `drop database pgeos_*` on `postgres`, and `bash database/schema/apply.sh` are not affected. 13 cases added to `tests/hooks/run.sh` (CI gate ①, `pnpm test:hooks`). Applies to the Master session too — shared-DB cleanups are run by the GM from his terminal.
- Finding 12 closes by this record; 2.11 part 1's review continues on the other findings (D-183 item 4).
- Model: claude-fable-5-1 (Master) · Delegated: none · ~12k tokens.
